> **Update (v2, 2026-10-20):** the revised draft annotations under the stricter expansion/compaction definition are in `revised/`, and the summary is in `REPORT_v2.md`. The v1 "138 missed radial events" finding is withdrawn: only 16 meet the strict definition.

# Annotation review — SchoolMotionClassifier real-data labels

Generated by `annotation_review/` scripts (read-only use of the repo's `src.features`, `src.labels` and `src.sim.io`). 946 annotated segments across 10 datasets were each evaluated; no sampling. Frames are 0-based at 29.97 fps, converted with the repo's own `mmss_to_frame`.

## 1. Overall verdict

**The labels themselves are largely sound; the boundaries and the coverage of expansion/compaction are where the problems are.**

- Label verdicts: **763 agree, 182 questionable, 1 disagree** (of 946). Stable labels: traveling, shoaling and milling segments sit in the expected Tunstrom regime or an adjacent intermediate one. Not one segment sits squarely in a *different* regime. All 194 expansion/compaction segments show the annotated sign of spread change, with radial velocity and convex hull agreeing (193 agree, 1 weak/mixed).
- The main label-level problem is **inconsistency, not wrong labels**. 138 robust, strong spread-change events (|Δlog R| ≥ 0.29, the median size of the annotated compactions; ≥60% of fish moving in/out; NND and median distance agree; no tracking teleports) are **not annotated**. 100 of them are isotropic (both principal axes change). They sit almost entirely in the 10- and 30-fish datasets, mostly inside `traveling` segments, whereas in the 70/150-fish datasets ~89% of such events are annotated. The same kind of event therefore gets labelled in big groups and skipped in small ones.
- **Boundaries are imprecise at the 0.5–1.5 s scale.** Only 91 segments have both boundaries supported within ±0.5 s. 232 segments have a boundary shift that two independent methods agree on, or a transition placed outside the actual state change. The most systematic issue is with transitions into compaction/expansion: in 54/109 `traveling → compaction` transitions the contraction is already under way before the transition starts (median -1.0 s).
- Tracking quality cannot explain any annotated expansion/compaction. Even the 12 that overlap tracking teleports pass outlier-robust checks.
- Metrics can't see intent or context. 'Questionable' means *look at the video*, not 'wrong'. Real milling is weaker than the Tunstrom 0.65 cut-off (median M = 0.61), so most milling segments land in 'questionable' even though every one of them resembles simulated milling.

## 2. Methods and thresholds

Per-frame quantities (all in `cache/<id>.npz`):

- Repo features, imported from `src.features.order_params.compute_order_params_series`: Φ_trans (= polarization P), Φ_tan, Φ_rad±, Φ_tan^unsigned, Φ_local (k=5). Repo 5-frame windows come from `src.features.windows.window_feature_matrix`.
- Added: Tunstrom rotation order M = |⟨r̂_i × v̂_i⟩|, mean radial velocity relative to the centroid (normalised by mean speed), mean distance to centroid R and d log R/dt, convex-hull area, mean nearest-neighbour distance (NND), mean speed, and the number of spatial clusters (single linkage at 6× median NND, every 5th frame).
- Smoothing: 15-frame (0.5 s) centred moving average for state classification.

| label | agree if | disagree if | source |
|---|---|---|---|
| traveling | ≥50% of frames with P>0.65 and M<0.35 | ≥50% of frames in another Tunstrom state | Tunstrom et al. 2013 |
| milling | ≥50% frames M>0.65 and P<0.35 | same | Tunstrom et al. 2013 |
| shoaling (swarm) | ≥50% frames P<0.35 and M<0.35 | same | Tunstrom et al. 2013 |
| expansion | Δlog R ≥ +0.10 over segment (first vs last 5 frames) and mean radial velocity > 0 | Δlog R<0, v_rad<0 and hull not growing | chosen; hull, NND, Φ_rad reported as extra evidence |
| compaction | Δlog R ≤ −0.10 and v_rad < 0 | mirror | same |
| transitions A→B (state→state) | A score falls and B score rises by >0.05 (pre: 1 s before + first 30%; post: last 30% + 1 s after) | both move the wrong way by >0.05 | scores: P, M, 1−max(P,M) |
| transitions involving exp/comp | d log R/dt changes the right way by >0.02 s⁻¹ across the segment; the stable side's score must be ≥0.5 | opposite change by >0.02 s⁻¹ |  |

Everything that is neither agree nor disagree is **questionable**. For stable labels that means an intermediate regime (e.g. P ≈ 0.5). A stable segment that agrees but contains an unannotated robust expansion/compaction event is downgraded to questionable.

**Threshold sensitivity** (final verdict counts under each threshold set; the spread threshold for exp/comp is in brackets):

| threshold set | agree | questionable | disagree |
|---|---|---|---|
| Tunstrom 0.65/0.35 [0.10] (used) | 763 | 182 | 1 |
| 0.60/0.40 [0.05] | 786 | 159 | 1 |
| 0.70/0.30 [0.20] | 696 | 249 | 1 |
| 0.55/0.45 [0.05] | 811 | 134 | 1 |

Only the agree/questionable split moves with the thresholds; the single disagree is stable. Milling is the most threshold-sensitive class.

## 3. Boundaries

Two independent methods were run on every segment start and end (1,892 sides, 1,024 unique boundaries):

1. **Change points.** `ruptures.KernelCPD` (linear kernel) on robust-standardised 5-frame window means of [P, M, Φ_rad±, d log R/dt]. The penalty is bisected so that the number of change points is 0.5×, 1× or 2× the number of annotated boundaries, at 3 smoothings (5, 15, 31 frames): 9 configs per dataset.
2. **Label criterion crossing.** The frame nearest the boundary (±3 s) where the label's own criterion crosses its midpoint: P = 0.5 (tpol), M = 0.5 (milling), max(P,M) = 0.5 (shoaling), or the sign of d log R/dt (exp/comp). Computed at 3 smoothings. Transition segments borrow the criterion of the stable neighbour that shares the boundary.

Status: *supported* = either method within ±0.5 s (the annotation grid is 0.5 s: 99.5% of timestamps fall on .0/.5). *shift_suggested* = both methods agree on a shift >0.5 s (to within 0.5 s); the suggestion is their mean. *shift_possible* = only one method is consistent. *unclear* = neither.

- Segment sides: {'supported': 671, 'unclear': 379, 'shift_possible(criterion)': 354, 'shift_possible(changepoint)': 330, 'shift_suggested': 158}.
- Unique boundaries: {'supported': 367, 'unclear': 205, 'shift_possible(criterion)': 194, 'shift_possible(changepoint)': 173, 'shift_suggested': 85}.
- **Chance level**: a random frame lands within ±0.5 s of a base-config change point 8%–19% of the time (mean 12%); annotated boundaries do so 30% of the time. The annotations are clearly better than random but far from frame-precise, so treat single-method 'possible shifts' as weak hints.
- **Boundary sensitivity**: the criterion-crossing offset moves by a median of 4 frames across smoothings (90th pct 28). The change-point offset moves by a median of 15 frames across the 9 configs (90th pct 80). Suggested shifts under ~0.5 s are therefore inside method noise. Only `shift_suggested` (two methods) is listed as a recommendation.
- **Transition placement** (where the state variable actually crosses relative to each transition segment, ±0.25 s tolerance): {'inside': 279, 'before': 85, 'after': 45, 'no_crossing_within_3s': 25}.

## 4. Expansion / compaction coverage (missed events)

Data-detected radial events: |d log R/dt| > 0.06 s⁻¹ for ≥1 s, |Δlog R| ≥ 0.10, radial velocity of matching sign. *Robust* = ≥60% of fish individually move in/out, NND and median distance to centroid change with the same sign, and no tracking teleports. *Strong* = |Δlog R| ≥ 0.29. *Isotropic* = both principal axes of the group change in the same direction; anisotropic events are mostly elongation into a file at travel onset (see `figures/segments/0105_unannotated_expansion_12441-12546.png`).

| dataset | n_fish | minutes | annotated_exp_comp | robust_strong_events | of_which_unannotated | frac_events_annotated | annotated_per_min | events_per_min |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0066 | 10 | 13.70 | 6 | 39 | 33 | 0.15 | 0.44 | 2.85 |
| 0084 | 30 | 13.70 | 23 | 43 | 21 | 0.51 | 1.68 | 3.14 |
| 0103 | 70 | 13.70 | 33 | 11 | 1 | 0.91 | 2.41 | 0.80 |
| 0105 | 10 | 13.70 | 12 | 43 | 32 | 0.26 | 0.88 | 3.15 |
| 0107 | 70 | 13.70 | 14 | 1 | 0 | 1.00 | 1.02 | 0.07 |
| 0115 | 30 | 13.70 | 20 | 23 | 12 | 0.48 | 1.46 | 1.68 |
| 0120 | 30 | 13.70 | 33 | 22 | 12 | 0.45 | 2.42 | 1.61 |
| 0124 | 70 | 13.60 | 11 | 0 | 0 |  | 0.81 | 0.00 |
| 0126 | 10 | 13.70 | 13 | 37 | 26 | 0.30 | 0.95 | 2.70 |
| 0200 | 151 | 10.00 | 29 | 9 | 1 | 0.89 | 2.90 | 0.90 |

138 robust strong events are unannotated (100 isotropic). Of the annotated expansion/compaction segments, 81% overlap a detected same-sign event, so the detector and the annotator mostly agree where both look. The gap is that in the 10-fish datasets 0066, 0105 and 0126, 70–85% of such events are not labelled. Largest isotropic missed events:

Caveat: with 10 fish, a single fish drifting or the group lining up can change R by 30–50% without any startle-like expansion, and the scheme does not say how large or how 'collective' a spread change has to be before it counts. Decide on a rule, then apply it the same way to every group size.

## 5. Gaps, overlaps, short segments, unannotated stretches

- Overlaps: 0. Segments extending past the data: 0.
- Gaps: 73 unannotated stretches (≥1 frame) between or after segments, total 758 s. Per dataset, 4%–18% of frames are unannotated. 75%–97% of unannotated frames are in the Tunstrom intermediate region, so you appear to skip ambiguous periods deliberately. That is fine, but it should be written down.
- Gaps where a clear behaviour occurs (≥1 s and either ≥60% of frames in one Tunstrom state or |Δlog R| ≥ 0.2): 33. The clearest ones:

- Short segments: 62 segments under 1 s, all of them transitions. None is dropped by `load_real_segments` (it only skips < int(0.5·fps) = 14 frames), but `build_real_xy(min_frames=15)` and `calibrate_baselines` **silently drop** 3 segments of 14 frames: 0115 seg 28 (expansion_to_traveling), 0120 seg 145 (traveling_to_compaction), 0126 seg 2 (traveling_to_compaction). Transitions have a median length of 1.5 s, and 44% are ≤1 s (≤6 five-frame windows). That is about the same size as the ±0.25 s rounding of the annotation timestamps.

## 6. Data sanity (tracking)

- No NaN positions in any dataset. Velocities are NaN only on the last frame of every file (no effect on the analysis). Frames are contiguous, and CSV and H5 are identical (checked on 0066).
- Exactly linear position runs (interpolated gap fills) make up 0.8–2.4% of fish-frames. Held (frozen) positions are rare except in 0200, where every one of the 151 tracks has 9–66 frozen frames.
- Large one-frame steps above max(10× median step, 99.9th pct): in the 30/70-fish datasets almost all are backed by the velocity column, so they are real fast moves (startles). **Teleports** (velocity does not support the step) cluster in the 10-fish datasets (51–53 each in 0066/0105/0126), consistent with identity swaps or re-acquisition.
- Group-level order parameters are permutation-invariant, so identity swaps barely move them. Teleports cause one-frame spikes in R and hull. All 12 annotated exp/comp segments that overlap a teleport still pass outlier-robust checks (median distance to centroid and per-fish radial velocity), so **none of the annotated expansion/compaction is a tracking artefact**.
- `150_fish/0200` has **151** tracks. Group splitting (more than one cluster) is common in 0120 (24% of frames) and 0084/0107 (~10%). Splits inflate R and can look like expansion; segments with >30% split frames are noted in the CSV.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0066 | 54 | 9 | 44 | 41 | 0.02 | 95 |
| 0084 | 110 | 1 | 237 | 11 | 0.01 | 655 |
| 0103 | 152 | 9 | 180 | 0 | 0.02 | 2390 |
| 0105 | 65 | 7 | 56 | 51 | 0.02 | 117 |
| 0107 | 72 | 11 | 128 | 2 | 0.02 | 2328 |

Nearest-label-centroid check, using z-scored (P, M, Φ_rad±, d log R/dt) segment means pooled over all datasets (rows = annotated label):

| label | compaction | expansion | milling | shoaling | traveling |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| compaction | 121 | 0 | 0 | 1 | 11 |
| expansion | 0 | 48 | 3 | 3 | 7 |
| milling | 0 | 0 | 36 | 0 | 0 |
| shoaling | 1 | 0 | 2 | 49 | 0 |
| traveling | 5 | 10 | 2 | 1 | 212 |

- Labels are used consistently across group sizes for the stable states: traveling P is 0.73–0.89, milling M is 0.60–0.68, shoaling P and M are both around 0.2–0.3. Large groups (70 fish) show systematically lower P while traveling (0.73) and during compaction (0.68). That is a group-size effect, not a labelling one.
- Expansion is the least self-consistent label: in a 7-nearest-neighbour check against segments from *other* datasets, only 62% of an expansion segment's neighbours are also expansion (tpol 0.90, comp 0.89, milling 0.88, shoaling 0.84). 7 expansion segments are nearer the traveling centroid, and 10 traveling segments are nearer the expansion centroid. The boundary between 'travel with some spreading' and 'expansion' is not applied uniformly.
- Annotated expansion/compaction magnitudes are similar across group sizes (median |Δlog R| 0.25–0.46), but the annotation *rate* of detected events is very different (see §4).

## 8. Resemblance to simulated classes

`sim_datasets/` is empty on this machine, so a small reference set was generated with the repo's own `scripts/generate_sims.py --out annotation_review/sim_ref --n-values 10 30 70 --seeds 4` (current YAML configs, 59/60 valid clips). A QDA classifier was fitted on the repo's 5-frame window features (5-fold CV accuracy on sim windows 0.65, not grouped by clip, so optimistic). Each real segment's windows were classified; the majority class is `sim_nearest` in the CSV.

| label | compaction | expansion | milling | shoaling | traveling |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| compaction | 84 | 0 | 3 | 1 | 45 |
| expansion | 0 | 26 | 2 | 5 | 28 |
| milling | 0 | 0 | 36 | 0 | 0 |
| shoaling | 0 | 0 | 1 | 51 | 0 |
| traveling | 17 | 12 | 1 | 3 | 197 |

Simulated window means (repo features):

| label | phi_trans_mean | phi_tan_mean | phi_rad_pm_mean | phi_tan_unsigned_mean | phi_local_mean |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| compaction | 0.69 | 0.17 | -0.19 | 0.50 | 0.79 |
| expansion | 0.59 | 0.29 | 0.30 | 0.57 | 0.75 |
| milling | 0.28 | 0.66 | -0.01 | 0.75 | 0.63 |
| shoaling | 0.32 | 0.19 | 0.00 | 0.57 | 0.54 |
| traveling | 0.86 | 0.31 | 0.03 | 0.51 | 0.92 |

Milling and shoaling map almost perfectly onto their simulated classes. About a third of real compaction and half of real expansion windows look like simulated *traveling* rather than simulated compaction/expansion. That says as much about the simulator (sim compaction/expansion are themselves fairly polarized) as about the labels.

## 9. Assessment of the annotation scheme

| family | n | total_s | median_s | min_s | n_lt_1s | n_windows_median | share_time |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| compaction | 133 | 528.52 | 4.00 | 1.50 | 0 | 24.00 | 0.07 |
| expansion | 61 | 215.37 | 3.00 | 1.50 | 0 | 18.00 | 0.03 |
| milling | 36 | 729.59 | 12.74 | 3.50 | 0 | 76.00 | 0.10 |
| shoaling | 52 | 408.43 | 5.51 | 2.00 | 0 | 33.00 | 0.06 |
| transition | 434 | 1025.95 | 1.50 | 0.47 | 62 | 9.00 | 0.14 |
| traveling | 230 | 4316.02 | 12.51 | 1.00 | 0 | 75.00 | 0.60 |

- **Fit to the data.** The five states exist in the data and the stable labels are applied consistently. But 12%–62% of frames per dataset (0124: 62%) are in *no* Tunstrom state, and 9%–56% of *annotated* frames are intermediate. The scheme forces these into one of five classes. An explicit `mixed/unclear` label (or confidence flag) would make the deliberate gaps and the weak milling/shoaling segments honest training targets.
- **Expansion/compaction are not exclusive of traveling.** Annotated compactions have median P = 0.78 and 95% polarized frames, so they are 'traveling while contracting'. A two-axis scheme would fit the data better: state (tpol/mill/shoal/mixed) × radial (expanding/stable/contracting). It would also remove most of the 20 transition types, and it matches how the simulator already treats `w_rad` as a separate knob.
- **Transitions.** 434 transition segments make up 14% of annotated time. They are short (median 1.5 s, 44% ≤ 1 s, i.e. ≤ 6 windows), about the size of the 0.5 s timestamp grid. In 89% of `X→compaction` transitions the group is already contracting (median rate −0.035 s⁻¹ vs −0.085 in compaction), so these are effectively the onset of compaction. 18 of 20 possible transition types are used, several only 1–2 times (milling→shoaling, shoaling→compaction, milling→compaction, shoaling→milling), which is too few to learn.
- **Missing behaviours.** (a) Group splitting/fission (0120: 24% of frames have >1 cluster). (b) Elongation/re-formation into a file at travel onset (anisotropic spread change; 38 robust events). (c) Intermediate/'mixed' states. No resting/very-slow periods were found (speed < 25% of the median in <2% of frames).
- **Duration vs 5-frame windows.** Stable segments are long enough (median 18–76 windows). Transitions yield a median of 9 windows, and 62 segments yield ≤ 5.
- **Class balance.** traveling is 60% of annotated time. Milling has only 36 segments (one dataset, 0124, contributes 2 segments of 96 s and 54 s). Expansion has 61. The README claim that 'compaction has no real labels' is out of date: there are 133.
- **Pipeline consistency.** `annotated_features.csv` (used by `figures.ipynb`) is **stale**: only 2 of its 354 rows match a current annotation span (e.g. 0066 seg 0 is 0–869 now, 0–240 there). Regenerate it before drawing conclusions from it.

## 10. Per-dataset segment tables

Columns: seg = index in the JSON; frames = [start, end) after `mmss_to_frame`; verdict = label verdict; bnd = boundary verdict (ok / ok_one_side / possible_shift / shift / unclear); evidence = smoothed means (P, M), Tunstrom state shares, Δlog R, Δlog hull, normalised radial velocity, repo Φ_rad±, and the nearest simulated class. Every row is also in `segment_review.csv`, with ~70 more columns.

## 11. What could not be checked

- **No video.** Every judgement uses trajectories alone. Context such as stimuli, walls, a predator model, or the light is invisible here. For expansion vs elongation and for weak milling, the video is the arbiter.
- No ground truth for boundaries. Change-point and criterion methods disagree with each other at the ~0.5 s level, and they are only modestly better than chance.
- Arena geometry is unknown (no wall coordinates), so wall-induced milling or compression could not be separated out.
- Simulated signatures come from a small reference set (59 clips), not your full sim set, and the QDA resemblance is only indicative.
- Identity swaps are inferred from teleports; there is no ID-confidence channel in the data.

## 12. Most important fixes (ranked)

1. **Decide and apply one rule for expansion/compaction across group sizes.** 138 robust strong spread-change events are unannotated (100 isotropic). They are concentrated in 0066 (33), 0105 (32), 0126 (26), 0084 (21), 0115 (12) and 0120 (12), mostly inside `traveling` segments. Examples: 0126 frames 13811–13910 compaction (Δlog R −0.95), 0066 17094–17230 expansion (+0.84), 0126 13654–13801 expansion (+0.79), 0105 4492–4606 expansion (+0.73), 0066 9764–9846 compaction (−0.71, in a gap). Either label them, or document a size/isotropy threshold that excludes them.
2. **Move transitions into compaction/expansion earlier, or merge them into the event.** In 76 `X→compaction/expansion` transitions the contraction or expansion starts before the transition segment (median -1.0 s). Examples: 0120 seg 28 (2877–2892, change −2.9 s earlier), 0200 seg 41 (6459–6503, −2.6 s), 0124 seg 4 (7163–7223, −2.5 s), 0103 seg 1 (420–465, −2.4 s). The transition itself already shows contraction in 89% of cases.
3. **Regenerate `annotated_features.csv`.** It is out of sync with the JSON annotations (2/354 spans match), and `figures.ipynb` reads it.
4. **Lengthen or drop sub-second transitions.** 62 segments are < 1 s, and 3 (14 frames) are silently dropped by `build_real_xy(min_frames=15)`: 0115 seg 28, 0120 seg 145, 0126 seg 2. Use a minimum of ~1 s (≥ 6 windows), or label boundaries as points instead of segments.
5. **Add a `mixed/unclear` label, or a confidence flag, for intermediate regimes,** instead of forcing them. The longest questionable stable segments are 0124 seg 0 milling (96 s, M = 0.61 but only 28% of frames > 0.65), 0124 seg 1 milling (54 s), 0124 seg 39 traveling (30 s, P = 0.64), 0084 seg 107 milling (25 s, M = 0.47, P = 0.42: the weakest milling), 0124 seg 56 traveling (24 s, P = 0.60, looks like simulated shoaling) and 0066 seg 27 shoaling (19 s, P = 0.53).
6. **Fix the two-method boundary shifts > 1 s** (36 boundaries; table in §3). For example 0115 seg 89 end → 20238 (−2.7 s), 0120 seg 68 end → 12816 (−2.4 s), 0103 seg 136/137 boundary → 21528, 0115 seg 1 (expansion) end → 2417 (+1.6 s), 0120 seg 79 shoaling end → 14881 (+1.5 s).
7. **Review the one disagreement:** 0103 seg 81 `compaction to traveling` (12318–12348). The group *starts* contracting there (rate −0.01 → −0.07 s⁻¹, R 184 → 165). The compaction ends later than annotated; it looks like the preceding compaction should extend about 1 s.
8. **Annotate or mark the clear unannotated gaps:** 0200 16738–17577 (28 s, 61% swarm frames), 0103 18162–18521 (12 s, Δlog R +0.61), 0126 13816–13906 (compaction, −0.90), 0126 20604–20724 (expansion, +0.65), 0200 4211–4615 (+0.55).
9. **Housekeeping:** update the README ('compaction has no real labels' is false; there are 133). Note that 0200 has 151 tracks. Consider a two-axis label scheme (state × radial) and dropping transition types with < 5 examples.

## Files

- `segment_review.csv`: one row per segment, with every metric, both verdicts, the suggestion, sensitivity verdicts, quality counts and sim resemblance.
- `boundary_review.csv`: one row per segment side (CP offsets for 9 configs summarised, criterion offsets at 3 smoothings, status).
- `radial_events.csv`, `gaps_review.csv`, `transition_crossings.csv`, `cp_null_rates.csv`, `tables_*.csv`, `sim_signatures.csv`.
- `figures/timeline_<id>.png` (10). `figures/segments/*.png`: 5-panel snapshots (1 s before, start, middle, end, 1 s after) for every non-agree segment, one agree example per label per dataset, and the 4 largest unannotated events per dataset.
- Scripts, in run order: `inventory.py`, `load_check.py`, `compute_frames.py`, `teleports.py`, `sim_signatures.py` (after generating `sim_ref/`), `analyze.py`, `finalize.py`, `transition_check.py`, `consistency.py`, `merge_final.py`, `plots.py`, `report.py`. The venv is in `.venv` (system site-packages + `ruptures`).
